\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})\cong

\displaystyle\GW(k)[H_{1},H_{2},H_{3},U_{2},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}]/(I(k)\cdot(H_{1},H_{2},H_{3},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}),

\displaystyle\mathcal{J},e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}+H_{3}e_{1}e_{2}-e_{3}^{2},\frac{n}{2}H_{2}e_{2},U_{2}^{2}+2U_{2},(h,H_{1},H_{2},H_{3})\cdot U_{2},

\displaystyle U_{2}e_{1}-\frac{n}{2}H_{3}e_{2},U_{2}e_{2}-ne_{2},U_{2}e_{3}-\frac{n}{2}H_{1}e_{2})

where
\mathcal{J}
is the ideal of relations from
Corollary

5.2
,

\displaystyle H_{1}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}

\displaystyle\;h_{\mathcal{O}(1,0)}(1)\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0))

\displaystyle H_{2}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}

\displaystyle\;h_{\mathcal{O}(0,1)}(1)\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(0,1))

\displaystyle H_{3}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}

\displaystyle\;h_{\mathcal{O}(1,1)}(1)\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,1))

\displaystyle e_{1}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}

\displaystyle\eulercw(\mathcal{O}(-1,0))\in\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0))

\displaystyle e_{2}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}

\displaystyle\eulercw(\mathcal{O}(0,-1))\in\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(0,1))

\displaystyle e_{3}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}

\displaystyle\eulercw(\mathcal{O}(-1,-1))\in\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,1))

and
U_{2}\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O})
is the pullback of the class
U
introduced in
Definition

3.5
along the projection map
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}B\mu_{n}
.

Note that
h_{\mathcal{L}}(1)
equals

(0,2)\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})\subseteq H^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{L})\times_{\chowmodtwo^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})}\chow^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})

for all line bundles
\mathcal{L}
in
\Pic(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
.
The inclusion here is justified by
[
HW19
, Prop. 2.11]
if
\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
has no non-trivial
2
-torsion which is satisfied by
[
Tot14
, Theorem 2.10, Lemma 2.12]
.

Proof.

For odd
n
this computation is very similar to that of
B\mu_{n}
in
Theorem

3.8
:
The Chow-Witt ring of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
is isomorphic to that of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
modulo
ne_{2}
and the quadratic periodicity isomorphisms for
\mathcal{O}(0,1)\cong\mathcal{O}(0,n+1)\cong\mathcal{O}(0,(n+1)/2)^{\otimes 2}
and
\mathcal{O}(1,1)\cong\mathcal{O}(1,n+1)\cong\mathcal{O}(1,(n+1)/2)^{\otimes 2}
.

Consider the class

\displaystyle H_{2}=(0,2)

\displaystyle\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(0,1))

\displaystyle\subseteq H^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{O}(0,1))\times_{\chowmodtwo^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})}\chow^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})\,.

By
Proposition

2.28
the square periodicity isomorphism
\sqperiod
acts as the identity on the second factor, and on the first factor it maps
0
to
0
, thus it sends
H_{2}
to
h\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O})
.
Similarly,
H_{3}
maps to
H_{1}
.

As a consequence,
e_{2}
is mapped to
(n+1)/2H_{2}e_{2}
.
The latter is a generator of the free summand
\mathbb{Z}/n\langle H_{2}e_{2}
of
\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(0,0))
and would also serve as a
\GW(k)
-algebra generator of the total Chow-Witt ring, but in order to achieve a more readable presentation we will instead write down
e_{2}
.

For
\sqperiod(e_{3})\in\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0))
note that the reduction map to Chow groups sends
e_{3}
and thus also
\sqperiod(e_{3})
to
c_{1}+c_{2}
.
As we have computed in
Theorem

6.1
,

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0))

\displaystyle=\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{O}(1,0))/(n\cdot e_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}\langle e_{1}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{Z}\langle H_{3}e_{2}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{Z}\langle H_{2}e_{3}\rangle/(n\cdot e_{2},he_{1}+H_{3}e_{2}-H_{2}e_{3})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}\langle e_{1}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{Z}/n\langle H_{3}e_{2}\rangle

and the only element here whose image in the Chow group is
c_{1}+c_{2}
, is
e_{1}+(n+1)/2\cdot H_{3}e_{2}
.

With this one can simplify the relations inherited from
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
as follows.

Eqs.

7
and
8

\displaystyle\text{ are all equivalent to }H_{1}^{2}-2h.

\displaystyle he_{1}+H_{3}e_{2}-H_{2}e_{3}

\displaystyle=he_{1}+H_{1}e_{2}-h(e_{1}+\frac{n+1}{2}H_{1}e_{2})

\displaystyle=H_{1}e_{2}-\frac{n+1}{2}2H_{1}e_{2}

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{and analogously for the other relations from \lx@cref{creftype\lx@tilde refnum}{eq:relations-BGmxBGm-3}}

\displaystyle I(k)\cdot H_{2}=I(k)\cdot H_{3}

\displaystyle=I(k)\cdot H_{1}

\displaystyle I(k)\cdot e_{3}

\displaystyle=I(k)\cdot e_{1}+I(k)\cdot(n+1)/2H_{3}e_{2}\text{ follows from }I(k)e_{1}\text{ and }I(k)e_{2}

\displaystyle e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}+H_{1}e_{1}e_{2}-e_{3}^{2}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}+H_{1}e_{1}e_{2}-(e_{1}+\frac{n+1}{2}H_{3}e_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}+H_{1}e_{1}e_{2}-e_{1}^{2}-(n+1)e_{1}H_{1}e_{2}-(\frac{n+1}{2})^{2}4e_{2}^{2}=0

This completes the proof for odd
n
.

For even
n
, the relations in the Chow-Witt ring of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
are also inherited from
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
and since the Picard group of these two spaces are isomorphic there are no quadratic periodicity isomorphisms to be divided out.
The only thing left to determine are the relations involving
U_{2}
.

By construction,
hU_{2}=0
.
To compute
H_{1}U_{2}
, consider the reduction map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chowwitt^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\chow^{0}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}\langle H_{1}\rangle}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}

This sends the generator
H_{1}
to
2
, and since the target is torsion-free this map is injective.
It is also compatible with multiplication.
Since
\rho(U_{2})=0
by construction, it follows that
\rho(H_{1}U_{2})=0
and thus one concludes
H_{1}U_{2}=0
.
Analogous arguments show
H_{2}U_{2}=H_{3}U_{2}=0
.

To compute
U_{2}^{2}
recall that
U_{2}
is defined as the image of
U\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O})
as constructed in
Definition

3.5
under the ring homomorphism

\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\colon\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mu_{n})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})

induced by the projection
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}B\mu_{n}
on the second factor.
By
[
LM23
, Prop. 5.2.3]
, for
B\mu_{n}
there is a relation
U^{2}=-2U
.
This implies

U_{2}^{2}=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(U)^{2}=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(U^{2})=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(-2U)=-2U_{2}

over
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
.
The same argument yields

U_{2}e_{2}=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(U)\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(e)=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(Ue)=\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(ne)=ne_{2}

where
e
is the Euler class of the bundle
\mathcal{O}_{B\mu_{n}}(1)
(denoted
T
in the
[
LM23
]
).

For
U_{2}e_{1}
consider the reduction map

\rho\colon\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\chow^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})

which sends
U_{2}
and thus also
U_{2}e_{1}
to zero.
By
Theorem

6.1
the Chow-Witt group on the left is

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0))

\displaystyle\cong\chowwitt^{1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{O}(1,0))/nH_{3}e_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}\langle e_{1}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{Z}\langle H_{3}e_{2}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{Z}\langle H_{2}e_{3}\rangle/(H_{2}e_{3}-he_{1}-H_{3}e_{2},nH_{3}e_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}\langle e_{1}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{Z}/n\langle H_{3}e_{2}\rangle

and the Chow group on the right is
\mathbb{Z}\langle c_{1}\rangle\oplus\mathbb{Z}/n\langle c_{2}\rangle
by
[
Tot14
, Theorem 2.10, Lemma 2.12]
.
The reduction map
\rho
maps Euler classes to the corresponding Chern classes, thus the kernel of
\rho
contains only the two elements
0
and
n/2H_{3}e_{2}
.
To distinguish between these two, consider

U_{2}e_{1}e_{2}=\langle-1\rangle^{2}U_{2}e_{2}e_{1}=(U_{2}e_{2})e_{1}=ne_{2}e_{1}

which implies that
U_{2}e_{2}
cannot be zero, leaving only the possibility
U_{2}e_{1}=n/2H_{3}e_{2}
.
An analogous argument shows
U_{2}H_{3}=n/2H_{1}e_{2}
.
∎

21
Milnor-Witt
K
-Theory Groups in Non-Diagonal Bidegrees

The computation in the next section will require
H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},K^{\mathrm{MW}}_{i-1},\mathcal{L})
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
j<i
.
If
i\neq 0
or
\mathcal{L}
is not trivial, then
H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},K^{\mathrm{MW}}_{j},\mathcal{L})
vanishes.

Proof.

Consider again the localization sequence
Eq.

10

which for arbitrary bidegrees reads

\ldots\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{i-1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},K^{\mathrm{MW}}_{j-1},s_{0}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(0,n))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},K^{\mathrm{MW}}_{j},\mathcal{L})\\
\xrightarrow{\iota^{*}}H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},K^{\mathrm{MW}}_{j},\iota^{*}\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},K^{\mathrm{MW}}_{j-1},s_{0}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(0,n))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\ldots

The second and fourth terms are shown in
Lemma

5.5
to vanish - implying that the third term vanishes as well - except when
i=0
and
\mathcal{L}
respectively
s_{0}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(0,n)
are trivial.
∎

22
I^{j}
-Cohomology of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}

Korollar 6.4
.

If
n
is odd:

\displaystyle H^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{*})\cong

\displaystyle W(k)[e_{1}]/(I(k)e_{1})

If

is even:

\displaystyle H^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{*})\cong

\displaystyle W(k)[U_{2},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}](I(k)\cdot(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}),e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}-e_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle U_{2}^{2}+2U_{2},U_{2}\cdot(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}))

The symbols
U_{2}
,
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
,
e_{3}
are as in
Theorem

6.2
.

Proof.

Combine the computation of
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
from
Theorem

6.2
and the fact that the sequence

\chow^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})\xrightarrow{h_{\mathcal{L}}}\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{i},\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

is exact.

Using a similar argument as in
Corollary

3.10
we find that for
n
odd,
the images of the hyperbolic maps
h_{\mathcal{O}(0,0)}
and
h_{\mathcal{O}(1,0)}
are the submodules of
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
generated by
h
and
H_{1}
, respectively.
Dividing out
he_{2}=2e_{2}
also identifies the relation
ne_{2}
with
e_{2}
, therefore the latter will be identified with zero as well.

If
n
is even, the images of the hyperbolic maps are generated by
h
,
H_{1}
,
H_{2}
and
H_{3}
.
After dividing these out, the relation
\frac{n}{2}H_{2}e_{2}
disappears because it is a multiple of
H_{2}
.
The same holds for all of the relation ideal
\mathcal{J}
.

The statement then follows by adding up the computed cohomology groups and inheriting the ring structure from
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
.
∎

Bemerkung 6.5
.

If
n
is odd, both the Chow-Witt ring and the
I^{j}
-cohomology ring satisfy a Künneth isomorphism

\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m})\otimes_{\GW(k)}\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mu_{n})\cong\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})

as
\GW(k)
-algebras.
If
n
is even, however, the Künneth map is neither injective nor surjective:
Both the Chow-Witt and
I^{j}
-cohomology ring contain an element
e_{3}
that does not come from one of the factors, and a new relation
U_{2}e_{1}-(n/2)H_{3}e_{2}
respectively
U_{2}e_{1}
.

Comparing with singular cohomology, the Künneth map not being surjective is not surprising as the classical Künneth theorem also features an additional Tor-term when none of the factor spaces has all free cohomology groups.
The failure of injectivity, on the other hand, is unexpected as this can never happen in singular cohomology.
This can be explained by considering the real cycle class map

H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{j},\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{i}_{\mathrm{sing}}((B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})(\mathbb{R});\mathbb{Z}(\mathcal{L}))\,.

[
HWXZ21
, Theorem 5.7]
does not apply in this case since (our scheme approximations of)
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
are not cellular, but
[
Jac17
, Corollary 8.3]
shows that this is an isomorphism if
j\geq 2i+5
(choose the approximation
\mathbb{P}^{r}\times E_{n}^{r}
with
r\geq i+2
which has dimension
2r+1\geq 2i+5
to compute
H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{j},\mathcal{L})
).
Similarly to
Remark

3.11
, for higher powers of the fundamental ideal the product
U_{2}e_{1}
does not vanish but presents an additional free generator of the respective
I^{j}
-cohomology groups.

This differs from Chow theory, where the Künneth isomorphism holds for
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
by
[
Tot14
, Lemma 2.12]
.

Chapter 7
Chow-Witt Ring of
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}

Throughout this chapter let
m
and
n
be positive integers and
k
a perfect field of characteristic coprime to
2
,
m
and
n
.

The Picard group of
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
is isomorphic to its first Chow group which is computed in
[
Tot14
, Theorem 2.10, Lemma 2.12]
to be isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/m\times\mathbb{Z}/n
.
For line bundles on (scheme approximations of)
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
we adopt the usual notation

\mathcal{O}(s,t)=\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}(s)\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{B\mu_{n}}(t)\,.

We are going to use another instance of the localization sequence like in the previous sections, this time associated to the decomposition

\mathbb{P}^{q}\times E_{n}^{r}\xhookrightarrow{s_{0}}E_{m}^{q}\times E_{n}^{r}\xhookleftarrow{\iota}(E_{m}^{q}\times E_{n}^{r})\smallsetminus s_{0}(\mathbb{P}^{q}\times E_{n}^{r})\,.

Under the usual isomorphisms this reads as follows:

\ldots\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\chowwitt^{i-1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},s_{0}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(m,0))\xrightarrow{\eulercw(\mathcal{O}(m,0))}\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})\\
\xrightarrow{\iota^{*}}\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\iota^{*}\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},K^{\mathrm{MW}}_{i-1},s_{0}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(m,0))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\ldots

(11)

Further we will use a trick for degree
0
building on work of Hudson-Matszangosz-Wendt
[
HMW24
]
.
Note that the group
H^{0}(X,I^{0},\mathcal{O}_{X})
forms a subring of the total
I^{j}
-cohomology ring of
X
.

Lemma 7.1
.

The
\witt(k)
-algebra homomorphism

\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon H^{0}(B\mu_{m},I^{0},\mathcal{O})\otimes_{\witt(k)}H^{0}(B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{O})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{O})

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Recall from
Definition

2.21
that
H^{i}(X,\witt,\mathcal{L})\cong H^{i}(X,I^{0},\mathcal{L})
, thus in degree
0
results about Witt cohomology can be applied.
In the following all groups have trivial twist and we will omit the twist from notation.

Consider the following diagram of schemes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{q}\times E_{n}^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{q}}(m)\times E_{n}^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{q}}(m)\smallsetminus s_{0})\times E_{n}^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{P}^{q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{q}}(m)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{q}}(m)\smallsetminus s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

and the
\witt(k)
-module homomorphism
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\colon H^{0}(B\mu_{n},\witt)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{q}},\witt)
.
The ladder lemma of
[
HMW24
, Lemma 4.5]
then states that the long exact localization sequence of the bottom row tensored with
H^{0}(E_{n}^{r},\witt)
and that of the top row form a commutative diagram of
\witt(k)
-modules:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{-1}(\mathbb{P}^{q},\witt)\otimes_{\witt(k)}H^{0}(E_{n}^{r},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{-1}(\mathbb{P}^{q}\times B\mu_{n},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{q},\witt)\otimes_{\witt(k)}H^{0}(E_{n}^{r},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{q}\times B\mu_{n},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(B\mu_{m},\witt)\otimes_{\witt(k)}H^{0}(E_{n}^{r},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{q},\witt)\otimes_{\witt(k)}H^{0}(E_{n}^{r},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{q}\times B\mu_{n},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{q},\witt)\otimes_{\witt(k)}H^{0}(E_{n}^{r},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{q}\times B\mu_{n},\witt)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\vdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{0})_{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{0}\times\mathrm{id})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\iota\times\mathrm{id})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\partial^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{0})_{*}\otimes\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s_{0}\times\mathrm{id})_{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}\cdot\mathrm{pr}_{2}}

By our previous computation in
Corollary

3.10

H^{0}(E_{n}^{r},\witt)\cong H^{0}(B\mu_{n},\witt)
is a free and therefore flat
\witt(k)
-module, hence the left-hand column is exact.
The first, second, fourth and fifth horizontal maps are isomorphisms by the Künneth formula for Witt cohomology
[
HMW24
, Theorem 4.7]
; the scheme
\mathbb{P}^{q}
is cellular and
H^{0}(B\mu_{n},\witt)\cong\witt(k)\langle 1,U\rangle
(as computed in
Corollary

3.10
) as well as
H^{>0}(B\mu_{n},\witt)\cong 0
(as computed in
Lemma

3.9
) are free
\witt(k)
-modules.
Thus by the five-lemma, the third horizontal map is an isomorphism as well.
∎

23
Group Structure

Satz 7.2
.

Let
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathcal{L}
a line bundle over
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
.

(i) If
m
and
n
are odd:

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\cong\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})/me_{1}

\displaystyle\cong\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{L})/(me_{1},ne_{2})

(ii) If
m
is odd and
n
even:

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\cong\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})/me_{1}

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\GW(k)\oplus\witt(k)\langle U_{2}\rangle&$i=0$, $\mathcal{L}$ trivial\\
\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{L})/(me_{1},\frac{n}{2}H_{2}e_{2})&else\end{cases}

If
m
is even and
n
odd, exchanging
m
and
n
reduces to case (ii).

(iii) If
m
and
n
are both even:

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\cong\begin{cases}\GW(k)\oplus\witt(k)\langle U_{1},U_{2},U_{1}U_{2}\rangle&$i=0$, $\mathcal{L}$ trivial\\
\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m})/(\frac{m}{2}H_{1}e_{1},\frac{n}{2}H_{2}e_{2})&else\end{cases}

Proof.

Assume first that
m
is odd.
Then
Lemma

6.3
shows that the last term of
Eq.

11
vanishes except when
i=0
and
s_{0}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(m,0)
is trivial in
\Pic(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})/2

which is the case for
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}(1,0)
.
This case however can be circumvented by using that in
\Pic(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})/2
,
\mathcal{O}(1,0)\sim\mathcal{O}(0,0)
and thus there is an isomorphism

\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(1,0))\cong\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O}(0,0))\,.

Therefore

\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})\cong\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})/\eulercw(\mathcal{O}(m,0))\cdot\chowwitt^{i-1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(m,0))

for all line bundles
\mathcal{L}
and
i\in\mathbb{Z}
.
This solves cases (i) and (ii).

(iii) In this case the last term of the localization sequence
11
vanishes and there is again an isomorphism

\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})\cong\chowwitt^{i}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})/\eulercw(\mathcal{O}(m,0))\cdot\chowwitt^{i-1}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(m,0))

except for
i=0
and
s_{0}^{*}\mathcal{L}\otimes\mathcal{O}(m,0)
trivial, i.e.
\mathcal{L}
trivial.

For this remaining case,
Lemma

7.1
shows

\displaystyle H^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{O}(0,0))

\displaystyle\cong H^{0}(B\mu_{m},I^{0},\mathcal{O}(0,0))\otimes_{\witt(k)}H^{0}(B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{O}(0,0))

\displaystyle=\witt(k)\langle 1,U_{1}\rangle\otimes_{\witt(k)}\witt(k)\langle 1,U_{2}\rangle

as
\witt(k)
-algebras.
To compute the Chow-Witt group, apply Hornbostel-Wendt’s fiber product formula
Proposition

2.22
:
From
[
Tot14
, Theorem 2.10, Lemma 2.12]
deduce
\chow^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})\cong\mathbb{Z}\langle 1\rangle
.
The kernel of the boundary map

\partial_{\mathcal{O}(0,0)}\colon\chow^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{1}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{1},\mathcal{O}(0,0))

is the whole domain as it follows from
Lemma

4.7
that
\partial_{\mathcal{O}(0,0)}=\bockstein_{\mathcal{O}(0,0)}\circ(\operatorname{mod}2)
maps
1
to
1\cdot\euleri(\mathcal{O}(0,0))=0
.
The structure map from
\chow^{0}
to
\chowmodtwo^{0}
is the usual mod
2
map.
The structure map from
H^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{O}(0,0))=\witt(k)\langle 1,U_{1},U_{2},U_{1}U_{2}\rangle
sends
1
to
1
, and
U_{1}
,
U_{2}
and thus also
U_{1}U_{2}
to zero as di Lorenzo-Mantovani have already shown in their work on the Chow-Witt ring of
B\mu_{n}

[
LM23
, Remark 5.2.2]
.
Therefore the Chow-Witt group in degree
0
evaluates to

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle\cong H^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{O})\times_{\chowmodtwo^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})}\chow^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})

\displaystyle=\witt(k)\langle 1,U_{1},U_{2},U_{1}U_{2}\rangle\times_{\mathbb{Z}/2}\mathbb{Z}\langle 1\rangle

\displaystyle=\GW(k)\langle 1\rangle\oplus\witt(k)\langle U_{1},U_{2},U_{1}U_{2}\rangle\,.\qed

Ring Structure

Satz 7.3
.

Let
m
and

be positive natural numbers.

(i) If
m
and
n
are odd:

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

(ii) If

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

\displaystyle\GW(k)[U_{2},H_{2},e_{1},e_{2}]/(I(k)\cdot(H_{2},e_{1},e_{2}),me_{1},\frac{n}{2}H_{2}e_{2},

\displaystyle H_{2}^{2}-2h,hU_{2},H_{2}U_{2},U_{2}^{2}+2U_{2},U_{2}e_{1},U_{2}e_{2}-ne_{2})

(iii) If
m
and

are even:

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

\displaystyle I(k)\cdot(H_{1},H_{2},H_{3},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}),\mathcal{J},e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}+H_{3}e_{1}e_{2}-e_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle(h,H_{1},H_{2},H_{3})\cdot(U_{1},U_{2}),U_{1}^{2}+2U_{1},U_{2}^{2}+2U_{2},

\displaystyle U_{2}e_{1}-\frac{n}{2}H_{3}e_{2},U_{2}e_{2}-ne_{2},U_{2}e_{3}-\frac{m}{2}H_{1}e_{2})

Here
U_{1}
and
U_{2}
correspond to the pullbacks along the projections
\mathrm{pr}_{1}
and
\mathrm{pr}_{2}
of the class
U
defined in
Definition

3.5
.
The symbols
e_{i}
map to
\eulercw(\mathcal{L}^{\vee})\in\chowwitt^{1}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})
and
H_{i}
to

(0,2)\in\chowwitt^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})\subseteq H^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{0},\mathcal{L})\times_{\chowmodtwo^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})}\chow^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})

for
\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{O}(1,0),\,\mathcal{O}(0,1),\,\mathcal{O}(1,1)
, respectively.

Proof.

(i) In
Theorem

7.2
we have seen that all Chow-Witt groups of
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
are isomorphic to those of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
modulo
me_{1}
,
thus the Chow-Witt ring is isomorphic to that of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
modulo
me_{2}
and the quadratic periodicity isomorphism
\sqperiod
for
\mathcal{O}(1,0)\cong\mathcal{O}(m+1,0)\cong\mathcal{O}((m+1)/2,0)^{\otimes 2}
.

The same argument as in the proof of
Theorem

6.2
for
n
odd shows
\sqperiod(H_{1})=h
.
This implies that the generator
H_{1}
as well as the relation
I(k)H_{1}
from
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
become redundant in
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
.

For (ii) we can use an analogous argument, but now there are two quadratic periodicities to consider, namely
\sqperiod_{\mathcal{O}((m+1)/2,0)}
and
\sqperiod_{\mathcal{O}((m+1)/2,1)}
.
Again using the arguments from
Theorem

6.2
yields

\displaystyle\sqperiod_{\mathcal{O}((m+1)/2,0)}(H_{1})

\displaystyle=h

\displaystyle\sqperiod_{\mathcal{O}((m+1)/2,1)}(H_{3})

\displaystyle=H_{2}

\displaystyle\sqperiod_{\mathcal{O}((m+1)/2,1)}(e_{3})

\displaystyle=(m+1)/2H_{3}e_{1}+e_{2}\,.

Thus the generators
H_{1}
,
H_{3}
and
e_{3}
as well as the relations
H_{1}U_{2}
,
H_{3}U_{2}
,
e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}+H_{3}e_{1}e_{2}-e_{3}^{2}
and everything in the relation ideal
\mathcal{J}
from
Eqs.

7
,
8
and
9
except for
H_{2}^{2}-2h
become redundant.
The relation
U_{2}e_{1}-(n/2)H_{3}e_{2}
becomes
U_{2}e_{1}
.

(iii) In this case there are no quadratic periodicity isomorphisms to be divided out.
All relations from the Chow-Witt ring of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
are inherited.
Exchanging
m
and
n
further allows to inherit the product of
U_{1}
with
H_{1}
,
H_{2}
,
H_{3}
,
e_{1}
,
e_{2}
and
e_{3}
from
B\mu_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
The localization sequence
Eq.

11
forces the relation
\eulercw(\mathcal{O}(m,0))
which equals
(m/2)H_{1}e_{1}
.
The Künneth formula in degree
0
from
Lemma

7.1
shows that
U_{1}U_{2}
is
\witt(k)
-linearly independent of the generators
1
,
U_{1}
and
U_{2}

hence there are no additional relations for this product.
∎

I^{j}
-Cohomology of

Korollar 7.4
.

and

\displaystyle\cong W(k)

\displaystyle H^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

If

and

are even:

\displaystyle H^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

\displaystyle\cong\witt(k)[U_{1},U_{2},e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}]/(I(k)\cdot(e_{1},e_{2},e_{3}),e_{1}^{2}+e_{2}^{2}-e_{3}^{2},

\displaystyle U_{1}^{2}+2U_{1},U_{2}^{2}+2U_{2},U_{1}e_{1},U_{1}e_{2},U_{1}e_{3},U_{2}e_{1},U_{2}e_{2},U_{2}e_{3})

Proof.

Combine the computation of
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
from
Theorem

7.3
and the fact that the sequence

\chow^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})\xrightarrow{h_{\mathcal{L}}}\chowwitt^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{i},\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

is exact.

If
m
and
n
are odd, the image of the hyperbolic map
h_{\mathcal{O}(0,0)}
is the submodule of
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
generated by
h
.
Dividing this out identifies the relation
me_{1}
with
e_{1}
and
ne_{2}
with
e_{2}
.
This leaves only the group
\chowwitt^{0}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},\mathcal{O})/h\cong\GW(k)/h\cong\witt(k)
.

If
m
is odd and
n
is even, the images of the hyperbolic maps
h_{\mathcal{O}(0,0)}
and
h_{\mathcal{O}(0,1)}
are generated by
h
and
H_{2}
, respectively.
Dividing out
h
identifies
me_{1}
with
e_{1}
(since
m
is odd and
2e_{1}-he_{1}\in I(k)e_{1}
) causing
e_{1}
to become trivial, and all relations containing
h
or
H_{2}
vanish.

If
m
and
n
are even, the images of the hyperbolic maps are generated by
h
,
H_{1}
,
H_{2}
,
H_{3}
, respectively.
The relation
U_{1}e_{1}-me_{1}
becomes
U_{1}e_{1}
, as
me_{1}
is a multiple of
2e_{1}=he_{1}
and thus vanishes.
All relations containing
h
,
H_{1}
,
H_{2}
or
H_{3}
vanish.

The statement on ring structure follows by adding up the computed cohomology groups and inheriting the ring structure from
\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})
.
∎

Bemerkung 7.5
.

For
m
and
n
both odd there is again a Künneth isomorphism

\displaystyle\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

\displaystyle\cong\chowwitt^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

\displaystyle H^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}

\displaystyle\cong H^{\hbox to2.21pt{\vbox to2.21pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.10417pt\lower-1.10417pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{{}}{}{{{}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}
}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 1.25 0 C 1.25 0.69 0.69 1.25 0 1.25 C -0.69 1.25 -1.25 0.69 -1.25 0 C -1.25 -0.69 -0.69 -1.25 0 -1.25 C 0.69 -1.25 1.25 -0.69 1.25 0 Z M 0 0}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{*})

for both the Chow-Witt and the
I^{j}
-cohomology ring.
If
m
and
n
are both even this map is injective but not surjective because the right-hand side contains the class
e_{3}
that does not come from one of the factors.
If
m
is odd and
n
even, the map is surjective but not injective because there is a new relation
U_{2}e_{1}-(n/2)H_{2}e_{2}
respectively
U_{2}e_{1}
on the right-hand side.

Like in
Remark

6.5
the real cycle class map

H^{i}(B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n},I^{j},\mathcal{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H_{\mathrm{sing}}^{i}((B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n})(\mathbb{R});\mathbb{Z}(\mathcal{L}))

is not an isomorphism in all bidegrees but only for
j\geq 2i+6
.
In these bidegrees the product
U_{2}e_{1}
does not vanish but presents an additional generator of the
I^{j}
-cohomology group, making the Künnneth map injective just like the one for singular cohomology.

In Chow theory, for comparison, the Künneth isomorphism holds by
[
Tot14
, Lemma 2.12]
: the group
\mu_{m}
satisfies condition (ii) because
k
contains all
e
-th roots of unity where
e
is the exponent of
\mu_{n}
(even if it does not contain all
n
-th roots of unity).

To summarize, of the product spaces considered in this work only
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
with
n
odd and
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
with both
m
and
n
odd satisfy a Künneth isomorphism for Chow-Witt rings.
For
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
with
m
and
n
odd the Künneth map is surjective but not injective, for
\mathbb{P}^{q}\times\mathbb{P}^{r}
,
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m}
and
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
with
m
and
n
even it is injective but not surjective, and for
B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
with
n
even and
B\mu_{m}\times B\mu_{n}
with
m
odd and
n
even it is neither injective nor surjective.
At this point it is unclear to the author if this behavior can be predicted in any way.
